Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- verify: Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.\n- code-review: Review the current diff for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud). Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. {"parentUuid":"3ea25acb-9e6f-4b6f-b706-151f0a392d62","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a1bd755-fe55-49e4-aa04-1c9cb10eb027","agentId":"a477a9a919faf465c","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01DJUrxyewhS2w4ondcGxs3a","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# SIGIL spine bounded-growth — DESIGN BRIEF (distilled from the understand phase)\n2\t\n3\tThe SIGIL spine is an append-only, hash-chained, Ed25519-signed JSONL log at `~/.sigil/spine/spine.jsonl`\n4\t(93 MB / 43,412 records, growing). GOAL: bound its growth / per-file size / read latency **without\n5\tweakening tamper-evidence or the append-only guarantee**. 34 consumers across 464 call sites read it;\n6\t23 do FULL scans; 9 need old-seq resolution. Full raw analysis (invariants, per-consumer contract,\n7\ttamper-conflict, durability, critique) is in `/tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/tasks/w705miq65.output` — read it for detail. Code is at `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/spine/*` and `sigil/reuse/{chain,canonical,models}.py`.\n8\t\n9\t## The 19 invariants a design MUST preserve (condensed)\n10\t\n11\t1. **chain-anchored-from-genesis** — `verify_chain` seeds `prev=_GENESIS_PREV` and rejects entries[0] if\n12\t   its prev_hash != genesis. (It does NOT require entries[0].seq==0 — only prev==genesis + contiguity —\n13\t   so a rebased chain at seq K is valid IFF K's prev_hash is rewritten to a genesis-equivalent value.)\n14\t2. **strict-seq-contiguity** — seq[i]==seq[i-1]+1, no gaps/dupes. Prune may only remove a CONTIGUOUS\n15\t   PREFIX, never punch interior holes.\n16\t3. **seq-is-immutable-primary-key** — never renumber; entry_hash binds seq; parent_id/supersedes_id/\n17\t   cursors/graph anchors reference seq. Keep original seqs across any compaction.\n18\t4. **signed-head-entry_count-is-the-anti-truncation-gate** — `classify_head` flags `len(entries) <\n19\t   head.entry_count` as TAMPERING; entry_count is ABSOLUTE. THE central conflict: prune ⇒ shorter ⇒ reads\n20\t   as rollback. Any prune MUST re-sign a snapshot-aware head atomically.\n21\t5. **monotonic-last_seq anti-rollback** — tip seq never decreases (verify_head prev_highwater [dormant on\n22\t   checkpoint path]; SpineTailer._high_water in-memory). Front-prune keeps tip seq ⇒ safe; renumber ⇒\n23\t   false trip + real-truncation-indistinguishable.\n24\t6. **append-only / offsets-never-move / no-in-place-rewrite** — never rewrite in place; build-then-\n25\t   os.replace. In-place same-or-larger rewrite is UNDETECTED by the size-only index heuristic ⇒ silent\n26\t   wrong reads.\n27\t7. **torn-tail-truncate-before-append (BLOCK-1)** — must run on the ACTIVE append segment only; a sealed\n28\t   segment that ends torn will never be truncated by a future append ⇒ permanent false verify() gap\n29\t   (critique A2: rotation must run `_last_valid_boundary` truncate at SEAL time).\n30\t8. **fsync durability** — acked append fsync'd; head swapped via temp+fsync+os.replace+dir-fsync\n31\t   (`checkpoint._atomic_write_text`). Every rotation output must match this.\n32\t9. **index extend/rebuild correctness under external mutation** — detection is SIZE-ONLY today; must\n33\t   become IDENTITY/EPOCH-based (st_dev/st_ino + manifest generation), and segment-aware (key by\n34\t   (segment,offset)). Rotation must not read as an in-place shrink (which is a truncation attack).\n35\t10. **replay-stable content digest (ts excluded)** — any snapshot/manifest record must digest via the\n36\t    same `append()` content shape + canonical_json (ts excluded) or its binding check fails.\n37\t11. **two-layer unkeyed integrity vs the keyed signed head** — a compactor is a recompute-capable writer;\n38\t    verify() alone can't certify a compaction. The owner-signed head is the authority; compaction must be\n39\t    owner-key-gated.\n40\t12. **read-path presents ONE contiguous genesis chain** — entries()/iter_records(-1)/verify()/count()\n41\t    must transparently concatenate snapshot-front + sealed segments + active segment in seq order, no\n42\t    gap/dup at a seam, so all 34 consumers keep working unchanged.\n43\t13. **durable tip determines next_seq (no seq reuse)** — `_read_last_entry`/next_seq must resolve the tip\n44\t    from the ACTIVE segment (true global max seq).\n45\t14. **cross-writer serialization must cover the compactor** — spine_lock (RLock) + flock. CRITICAL: flock\n46\t    binds to the INODE, not the path; os.replace swaps the inode, so flock-on-target CANNOT exclude a\n47\t    cross-process appender. Need a PATH-STABLE lockfile (`spine.lock`) both append & compaction flock\n48\t    before resolving the active segment.\n49\t15. **monotonic-state-by-full-scan must survive** — per-device nonce high-water (envelope.py), newest\n50\t    warden_checkpoint per pubkey (cli.py), account.create cap count (actor_scope/actor), device\n51\t    authorize/revoke ledger (mesh/registry), kill-switch latch (killswitch), promotion grants\n52\t    (promotion), unresolved queued approvals (approvals), archivist supersession + promotion-key ledger\n53\t    (revise), open threads/commitments/contradictions + citations (queries.py). Rotation (retain-all)\n54\t    keeps these IF scans span segments; PRUNE would silently corrupt them unless each is folded into the\n55\t    signed snapshot.\n56\t16. **referential integrity of parent_id/supersedes_id across a prune** — never dangle; never mutate a\n57\t    retained record's parent/supersedes (breaks its binding).\n58\t17. **canonical/domain primitives frozen** — snapshot/head schema changes need a schema_version bump +\n59\t    migration, computed by the same reuse functions.\n60\t18. **single signed head is the commit point** — extend to carry a snapshot base coherently; keep ONE\n61\t    atomically-published head.\n62\t19. **genesis-vs-empty count semantics** — a compacted store is never \"empty\"; next_seq/last_seq must\n63\t    derive from the snapshot base, not physical len==0.\n64\t\n65\t## Critique additions (must be in any design)\n66\t- **A1 closed head schema** — `SignedChainHead`/`ChainEntry` are `extra=\"forbid\"`. A v2 head with snapshot\n67\t  fields is a BREAKING wire migration; today the two head-consumers fail DIFFERENTLY (SpineTailer swallows\n68\t  ⇒ silent un-anchored; verify_checkpoint has no try/except ⇒ crashes `sigil verify` + mcp ingest_status).\n69\t- **A3 next_seq is cached** — a long-lived store instance compacted by another process keeps stale\n70\t  next_seq; must invalidate on epoch change.\n71\t- **A4 size-as-change-token in THREE places** — store._ensure_index, killswitch.py:57, gesture/session.py:121.\n72\t  A same-size rotation serves a STALE kill-switch verdict in the un-halting direction. Fix must be UNIFIED\n73\t  (shared epoch token).\n74\t- **Missing consumers** — consolidate/queries.py (full-scan, citation-heavy MCP surface — first-class),\n75\t  consolidate/gate.py (get(seq) grounding), ingest/transcript.py (sets parent_id ⇒ referential integrity).\n76\t- **C1 (decisive)** — NO durable anti-rollback floor lives OUTSIDE the compactable data. Hard-prune turns\n77\t  the `n<signed_n` truncation alarm into a no-op for the pruned range, so a cold/fresh verifier can't\n78\t  distinguish a legit compaction-baseline from a replayed old (short-spine, low-count-head) pair. Closing\n79\t  this needs a durable external floor (per-verifier local last-accepted last_seq, and/or authenticated\n80\t  head delivery a mirror can't roll back). WARM same-host verifiers are fine; COLD/fresh are not.\n81\t- **C2** — once classify_head slices only the LIVE window, the pruned prefix is NO LONGER re-hashed, so the\n82\t  snapshot's committed digest MUST be a Merkle/hash commitment binding EVERY pruned record (else a pre-\n83\t  archival edit is unprovable). This is an invariant, not a nice-to-have.\n84\t- **D1** — must NOT hold the append lock across the O(file) heavy copy (would block a kill-switch PANIC\n85\t  append). Heavy copy of immutable records is lock-free; only a bounded seal→publish critical section\n86\t  takes the lock.\n87\t- **D2** — live-reader vs unlink race across the manifest indirection: readers must snapshot ALL segment\n88\t  fds right after reading the manifest; unlink deferred behind a reader grace/refcount.\n89\t\n90\t## Cutover protocol (durability analysis — the required shape)\n91\tONE authoritative **manifest** naming the active segment set = the sole linearization point, swapped via\n92\t`_atomic_write_text` (temp→fsync→os.replace→dir-fsync). Ordering: write+fsync new segment(s) → write+fsync\n93\tnew manifest → os.replace manifest (the atomic instant) → dir-fsync → ONLY THEN unlink superseded segments.\n94\tentries()/count() derive the set from the MANIFEST, never a directory glob (glob double-counts during the\n95\tunlink window). Manifest swap + head swap must be crash-consistent together (a crash between them yields a\n96\tfalse TAMPERING window). Seal-time torn-tail truncate on the sealed segment. Carry seq/entry_hash across\n97\tthe seam so verify_chain's contiguity+linkage never break.\n98\t\n99\t## The 5 questions the DESIGN must answer\n100\t1. Where does the durable, non-replayable anti-rollback floor live? (C1) — only relevant if PRUNING.\n101\t2. Exact SignedChainHead schema migration + version-negotiation (old mobile clients / sigil verify vs a v2 head)?\n102\t3. The atomic multi-file commit protocol + which bounded critical section holds the lock (D1/D2/D3)?\n103\t4. Which record classes are un-prunable state-bearers and how is each folded into the signed snapshot? (only if pruning)\n104\t5. Honest contract for resolving a pruned seq (positive \"compacted below N, see snapshot X\" vs a bare 404)?\n105\t\n106\t## Key strategic observation (for the panel to validate or challenge)\n107\tRetain-all SEGMENT ROTATION needs NONE of {C1 durable floor, snapshot state-folding, head-schema prune\n108\tsemantics} — it keeps entry_count absolute, the chain genesis-rooted, all 34 consumers transparent (via a\n109\tsegment-spanning read API), and reclaims practical disk via gzip-on-seal (JSONL ~5-10x). It touches only\n110\tstorage mechanics (manifest, lockfile, epoch-index, cutover) — the HARD-but-BOUNDED engineering. HARD-PRUNE\n111\t(space reclaim by deleting records) is where C1 + state-folding + a breaking head migration live — a\n112\ttamper-evidence tradeoff that is an OPERATOR decision, not an autonomous one.\n113\t"}]},"uuid":"ca4d7f37-382e-4ab7-81f8-06e80b4c750e","timestamp":"2026-07-19T17:43:42.305Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3ea25acb-9e6f-4b6f-b706-151f0a392d62","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Full\n161\t        # scans (verify/count/entries/iter_records(-1)) never touch the index, staying a single pass across\n162\t        # segments. Invalidation is EPOCH-based (`change_token`: manifest generation + active dev/ino/size),\n163\t        # so a rotation/migration is detected — a same-size in-place rewrite can no longer fool a size-only\n164\t        # heuristic (invariant 9). `_index_lock` guards the dicts against concurrent read+append.\n165\t        self._index_lock = threading.Lock()\n166\t        self._offsets: dict[int, tuple[str, int]] = {}   # seq -> (segment path str, byte offset in it)\n167\t        self._seg_scanned: dict[str, int] = {}           # segment path str -> bytes indexed so far\n168\t        self._index_epoch: tuple | None = None           # change_token when the index was last reconciled\n169\t        self._max_seq = -1\n170\t        self._last: ChainEntry | None = self._read_last_entry()\n171\t\n172\t    # --- segment layout / manifest ------------------------------------------------\n173\t    def _resolve_active_path(self) -> Path:\n174\t        \"\"\"The current append/read target. With a manifest, the active segment's absolute path; without\n175\t        one (a fresh or not-yet-migrated legacy spine), the legacy single file in place — byte-identical\n176\t        to the pre-rotation store, so nothing destructive happens until an explicit `migrate()`. A manifest\n177\t        that exists but names NO active segment is a corrupt/degenerate state and RAISES rather than\n178\t        silently falling back to `spine.jsonl` (which would resurrect a data file OUTSIDE the segment set —\n179\t        a fork; the guard also removes the same landmine for Slice-3 sealing, which must always leave an\n180\t        active segment).\"\"\"\n181\t        if self._manifest is not None:\n182\t            act = self._manifest.active()\n183\t            if act is None:\n184\t                raise SpineError(\"manifest has no active segment (corrupt/degenerate) — refusing to \"\n185\t                                 \"resurrect spine.jsonl outside the segment set\")\n186\t            return self._layout.seg_path(act)\n187\t        return self.path\n188\t\n189\t    def _read_target(self) -> Path:\n190\t        \"\"\"The file to read for THIS operation. Normally the cached active segment; if it has VANISHED (a\n191\t        migrate()/rotation in another process renamed it away), resolve the current active from the\n192\t        manifest for this read only — WITHOUT mutating shared state (lock-free reads must not race\n193\t        append's active update). Prevents a false-EMPTY read during the migrate instant, which for the\n194\t        nonce-highwater scan would regress the replay floor. In Slice 0 the only mover is migrate(), which\n195\t        RENAMES (byte-preserving), so the current index offsets stay valid for the resolved file. Fully\n196\t        epoch-invalidated, segment-spanning reads land in Slice 1.\"\"\"\n197\t        a = self._active\n198\t        if a.exists():\n199\t            return a\n200\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n201\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n202\t        if act is not None:\n203\t            r = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n204\t            if r.exists():\n205\t                return r\n206\t        # PRE-manifest migrate window: the atomic rename has already placed the data at seg-0 but the\n207\t        # manifest is not published yet (so `m is None`). Read it there — otherwise a lock-free reader in\n208\t        # exactly this instant sees a false-EMPTY spine (kill-switch un-halt / nonce-floor regression) and\n209\t        # the indexed read path can hit a bare FileNotFoundError. seg-0 is byte-identical (rename), so the\n210\t        # index built over the vanished file stays valid on it.\n211\t        if m is None:\n212\t            seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n213\t            if seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists():\n214\t                return seg0\n215\t        return a\n216\t\n217\t    def _segments_in_order(self) -> list[Path]:\n218\t        \"\"\"The segment files to read, in seq order (sealed by first_seq, then the active tail). With no\n219\t        manifest this is the single legacy/active file. Resolved fresh each call so a rotation/migration by\n220\t        another process is seen. RAISES SpineError on a manifest-referenced segment file that is MISSING —\n221\t        a read must never silently yield a short chain (invariant 15: no state-scanner fail-open).\"\"\"\n222\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n223\t        if m is None:\n224\t            t = self._read_target()\n225\t            return [t] if t.exists() else []\n226\t        out: list[Path] = []\n227\t        for seg in m.ordered():\n228\t            p = self._layout.seg_path(seg)\n229\t            if not p.exists():\n230\t                raise SpineError(\n231\t                    f\"spine manifest (generation {m.generation}) references a missing segment: {seg.file}\")\n232\t            out.append(p)\n233\t        return out\n234\t\n235\t    def _seam_tip_for_active(self) -> \"ChainEntry | None\":\n236\t        \"\"\"When the ACTIVE segment is empty, the chain tip its first append must extend so the seam stays\n237\t        contiguous: the prior SEALED segment's real last record (its boundary). None when the active is the\n238\t        GENESIS segment (first_seq == 0) — a genuinely fresh spine correctly starts at seq 0. Prevents a\n239\t        seq-0 fork across a rotation seam (invariant 13). MUST be consulted under the append lock (reads the\n240\t        manifest fresh). No-op in Slices 0-2 (the only segment is genesis).\"\"\"\n241\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n242\t        if m is None:\n243\t            return None\n244\t        act = m.active()\n245\t        if act is None or act.first_seq == 0:\n246\t            return None\n247\t        sealed = m.sealed_in_order()\n248\t        if not sealed:\n249\t            return None\n250\t        sp = self._layout.seg_path(sealed[-1])\n251\t        line = _last_nonempty_line(sp) if sp.exists() else None\n252\t        if not line:\n253\t            return None\n254\t        d = json.loads(line)\n255\t        return ChainEntry(seq=d[\"seq\"], prev_hash=d[\"prev_hash\"], cert_digest=d[\"cert_digest\"],\n256\t                          entry_hash=d[\"entry_hash\"])\n257\t\n258\t    @contextmanager\n259\t    def _crossproc_lock(self) -> \"Iterator[None]\":\n260\t        \"\"\"Cross-process exclusion on the inode-stable lockfile (invariant 14 / D3). flock binds to the\n261\t        open file DESCRIPTION, not the path, so an os.replace of the manifest/segments cannot move the\n262\t        lock out from under a concurrent writer — which the prior flock-on-the-data-fd could not survive.\n263\t        Best-effort exactly as before (fcntl absent on non-POSIX; a flock failure is swallowed). NOT\n264\t        re-entrant across fds — never nest a second `_crossproc_lock` inside one (a second LOCK_EX on the\n265\t        same file from a different fd self-deadlocks); the seal-swap that runs inside append reuses the\n266\t        already-held lock rather than re-acquiring.\"\"\"\n267\t        if fcntl is None:  # pragma: no cover — non-POSIX\n268\t            yield\n269\t            return\n270\t        self._layout.lockfile_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n271\t        fd = os.open(str(self._layout.lockfile_path), os.O_RDWR | os.O_CREAT, 0o600)\n272\t        try:\n273\t            try:\n274\t                fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX)\n275\t            except OSError:  # pragma: no cover\n276\t                pass\n277\t            yield\n278\t        finally:\n279\t            os.close(fd)\n280\t\n281\t    def _complete_orphan_migration_locked(self) -> bool:\n282\t        \"\"\"MUST hold `_crossproc_lock`, and be called only when the manifest is currently ABSENT. If we are\n283\t        in the migrate() crash window — seg-0 present, `spine.jsonl` gone (the rename is atomic + durable),\n284\t        no manifest — finish the interrupted migration by publishing the manifest. Returns True iff it\n285\t        completed one. Called from BOTH __init__'s reconciler AND append's `_refresh_active_under_lock`, so\n286\t        a LIVE appender completes the orphan under the lock instead of re-creating `spine.jsonl` and forking\n287\t        the log (the critical review finding).\"\"\"\n288\t        seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n289\t        if seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists():\n290\t            write_manifest(self._layout, initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=self._layout.segment_rel(0)))\n291\t            _log.warning(\"spine: completed an interrupted migration — published the manifest for %s\", seg0)\n292\t            return True\n293\t        return False\n294\t\n295\t    def _refresh_active_under_lock(self) -> None:\n296\t        \"\"\"MUST hold `_crossproc_lock`. FIRST complete any interrupted migration (so a live appender never\n297\t        resurrects `spine.jsonl`), THEN re-resolve the active segment from the manifest so an appender\n298\t        constructed before a concurrent migrate()/rotation writes to the CURRENT target — never a stale\n299\t        path (fork) or a renamed-away file (resurrection). One manifest read in the steady state.\"\"\"\n300\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n301\t        if m is None and self._complete_orphan_migration_locked():\n302\t            m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n303\t        new_active = self.path\n304\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n305\t        if act is not None:\n306\t            new_active = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n307\t        if new_active != self._active or (m is not None) != (self._manifest is not None):\n308\t            self._manifest = m\n309\t            self._active = new_active\n310\t            self._invalidate_index()\n311\t            self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n312\t\n313\t    def _invalidate_index(self) -> None:\n314\t        \"\"\"Drops the segment-aware index so the next read rebuilds it against the (possibly changed)\n315\t        segment set.\"\"\"\n316\t        with self._index_lock:\n317\t            self._offsets = {}\n318\t            self._seg_scanned = {}\n319\t            self._index_epoch = None\n320\t            self._max_seq = -1\n321\t\n322\t    def _reconcile_orphan_migration(self) -> None:\n323\t        \"\"\"Close the migrate() crash window at CONSTRUCTION. migrate() does an ATOMIC\n324\t        `os.replace(spine.jsonl → segments/seg-00000000.jsonl)` and THEN publishes the manifest; a crash\n325\t        BETWEEN them leaves seg-0 present, `spine.jsonl` gone, and no manifest — at which point the legacy\n326\t        fallback (active = spine.jsonl) would read the spine as EMPTY and silently hide all history. If we\n327\t        observe exactly that state, finish the (already-durable) migration under the lock. (A live appender\n328\t        does the same via `_refresh_active_under_lock`; a construction-time pass just recovers faster.)\"\"\"\n329\t        if self._manifest is not None:\n330\t            return\n331\t        seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n332\t        if not (seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists()):\n333\t            return\n334\t        with self._crossproc_lock():\n335\t            if read_manifest(self._layout) is None:          # re-check under the lock\n336\t                self._complete_orphan_migration_locked()\n337\t            self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n338\t\n339\t    def change_token(self) -> tuple:\n340\t        \"\"\"A cheap, rotation-aware epoch token — (manifest generation, active file, size, inode) — that\n341\t        changes on ANY append (size), a migration/rotation (generation + active path/inode), or a reset.\n342\t        THE unified replacement for the size-only 'has the spine changed?' heuristic at every site\n343\t        (invariant 9 / A4) — notably the kill-switch verdict caches in `governor/killswitch.py` and\n344\t        `gesture/session.py`. Because it reads the manifest FRESH and keys on the resolved active segment\n345\t        (not the legacy `store.path`, which a migration renames away), a same-size rotation — or a\n346\t        migration performed by another process — can never serve a stale (un-halting) kill-switch verdict.\"\"\"\n347\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n348\t        gen = m.generation if m is not None else -1\n349\t        active = self.path\n350\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n351\t        if act is not None:\n352\t            active = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n353\t        try:\n354\t            st = active.stat()\n355\t            return (gen, str(active), st.st_size, st.st_ino)\n356\t        except OSError:\n357\t            return (gen, str(active), -1, -1)\n358\t\n359\t    def migrate(self) -> bool:\n360\t        \"\"\"Move a legacy single-file spine into the segment layout: rename `spine.jsonl` →\n361\t        `<stem>.segments/seg-00000000.jsonl` (atomic, same filesystem — O(1), no byte copy) and publish a\n362\t        single-active-segment manifest. Idempotent (a no-op once a manifest exists). Returns True iff it\n363\t        migrated. SPLIT-BRAIN SAFETY: run this only after every spine writer is on code that flocks the\n364\t        path-stable lockfile (this Slice-0 code) and re-resolves the active segment under that lock — a\n365\t        stale appender then completes/observes the migration instead of resurrecting `spine.jsonl`. After\n366\t        migration `spine.jsonl` is absent and is NEVER re-created as a data file (all data I/O targets the\n367\t        active segment).\"\"\"\n368\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n369\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n370\t                if read_manifest(self._layout) is not None:\n371\t                    self._refresh_active_under_lock()\n372\t                    return False\n373\t                self._layout.segments_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n374\t                seg0_abs = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n375\t                if self.path.exists():\n376\t                    os.replace(self.path, seg0_abs)         # atomic rename (same fs); O(1), no copy\n377\t                    fsync_dir(self._layout.spine_dir)       # persist the SOURCE-dir unlink of spine.jsonl\n378\t                else:\n379\t                    seg0_abs.touch()                        # fresh spine: create the empty active segment\n380\t                fsync_dir(self._layout.segments_dir)        # persist the TARGET-dir creation of seg-0\n381\t                write_manifest(self._layout, initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=self._layout.segment_rel(0)))\n382\t                self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n383\t                self._active = self._resolve_active_path()\n384\t                self._invalidate_index()\n385\t                self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n386\t                return True\n387\t\n388\t    def reset(self) -> None:\n389\t        \"\"\"Delete EVERY spine artifact for this store — the legacy data file, the manifest, all segments,\n390\t        the trash, and the lockfile — for `sigil ingest --reset` (a full rebuild). Under the cross-process\n391\t        lock so it can't race an append. Idempotent; leaves the store readable (empty) afterward. Replaces\n392\t        the old `SPINE_PATH.unlink()`, which left the manifest + rotated segments behind (stale).\"\"\"\n393\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n394\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n395\t                for pth in (self._layout.manifest_path, self.path, self._layout.lockfile_path):\n396\t                    try:\n397\t                        pth.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n398\t                    except OSError:\n399\t                        pass\n400\t                for dpath in (self._layout.segments_dir, self._layout.trash_dir):\n401\t                    if dpath.exists():\n402\t                        shutil.rmtree(dpath, ignore_errors=True)\n403\t                self._manifest = None\n404\t                self._active = self._resolve_active_path()\n405\t                self._invalidate_index()\n406\t                self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n407\t\n408\t    def generation(self) -> int:\n409\t        \"\"\"The manifest generation (monotonic rotation epoch); -1 for a legacy (un-migrated) spine.\"\"\"\n410\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n411\t        return m.generation if m is not None else -1\n412\t\n413\t    def segment_info(self) -> list[dict]:\n414\t        \"\"\"A CLI/human summary of the segment set: one dict per segment in seq order. Empty list for a\n415\t        legacy (un-migrated) single-file spine.\"\"\"\n416\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n417\t        if m is None:\n418\t            return []\n419\t        out: list[dict] = []\n420\t        for seg in m.ordered():\n421\t            p = self._layout.seg_path(seg)\n422\t            out.append({\"id\": seg.id, \"codec\": seg.codec, \"sealed\": seg.sealed,\n423\t                        \"first_seq\": seg.first_seq, \"last_seq\": seg.last_seq,\n424\t                        \"bytes\": p.stat().st_size if p.exists() else 0, \"file\": seg.file})\n425\t        return out\n426\t\n427\t    # --- write --------------------------------------------------------------------\n428\t    def append(\n429\t        self, *, kind: str, source: str, actor: str, payload: dict[str, Any],\n430\t        parent_id: int | None = None, supersedes_id: int | None = None,\n431\t        ts: str | None = None,\n432\t    ) -> int:\n433\t        content = {\n434\t            \"scope\": SCOPE, \"kind\": kind, \"source\": source, \"actor\": actor,\n435\t            \"payload\": payload, \"parent_id\": parent_id, \"supersedes_id\": supersedes_id,\n436\t        }\n437\t        cert_digest = digest_payload(content)  # wallclock-free\n438\t        # Serialize the whole read-tip → write so concurrent writers (threaded bridge server, gesture\n439\t        # daemon) can't both fork off a stale tip and break the chain. The in-process RLock is keyed on\n440\t        # the STABLE `self.path` (unchanged), so the check-then-append gate `envelope.consume` builds on\n441\t        # top of it stays atomic across a rotation. Re-read the TRUE tip from disk under the lock.\n442\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n443\t            with self._crossproc_lock():                 # cross-process guard on the path-stable lockfile\n444\t                self._refresh_active_under_lock()        # pick up a concurrent migrate()/rotation; never fork\n445\t                active = self._active\n446\t                # binary append+read: lets us TRUNCATE a torn tail before writing (BLOCK-1 fix). `a+b`\n447\t                # creates the file if absent and — in append mode — every write still lands at EOF.\n448\t                with active.open(\"a+b\") as f:\n449\t                    # BLOCK-1: an interrupted write can leave torn bytes PAST the last valid record. Appending\n450\t                    # after them would MERGE (torn + new) into one unparseable line → the new record is silently\n451\t                    # lost, verify() stays green, and a lost kill-switch panic never halts the mesh. Truncate the\n452\t                    # dead tail back to the last valid record FIRST (this only removes never-committed garbage\n453\t                    # from an interrupted write; committed records are untouched). Evaluated on the ACTIVE\n454\t                    # segment under the lock (invariant 7 — the append target is always the true chain tip).\n455\t                    clean_end, last = _last_valid_boundary(active)\n456\t                    pre_size = os.fstat(f.fileno()).st_size\n457\t                    if pre_size > clean_end:\n458\t                        f.truncate(clean_end)\n459\t                        _log.warning(\"spine: truncated a %d-byte torn tail (interrupted write) before append (%s)\",\n460\t                                     pre_size - clean_end, active)\n461\t                    if last is not None:\n462\t                        entry = append_entry([last], cert_digest)          # extend the active segment's tip\n463\t                    else:\n464\t                        # active is EMPTY: seed from the prior sealed segment's boundary if this is a rotated\n465\t                        # (non-genesis) segment, else start the genesis chain at seq 0. Never build_chain a\n466\t                        # non-genesis segment from GENESIS (that would fork the chain at seq 0 — invariant 13).\n467\t                        seam = self._seam_tip_for_active()\n468\t                        entry = append_entry([seam], cert_digest) if seam is not None else build_chain([cert_digest])[0]\n469\t                    record = {\n470\t                        \"seq\": entry.seq, **content, \"ts\": ts or now_iso(),\n471\t                        \"cert_digest\": cert_digest, \"prev_hash\": entry.prev_hash, \"entry_hash\": entry.entry_hash,\n472\t                    }\n473\t                    line = json.dumps(record, ensure_ascii=False) + \"\\n\"\n474\t                    offset = clean_end                      # after any truncate, EOF == clean_end (where the line lands)\n475\t                    f.write(line.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n476\t                    f.flush()\n477\t                    os.fsync(f.fileno())                    # FIX 3: an ack'd append is durable across a crash\n478\t                    # FIX 1: keep the index current with NO re-scan when the ACTIVE segment is already\n479\t                    # indexed exactly up to this record's offset. If another PROCESS appended in between,\n480\t                    # `_seg_scanned[active] < offset` and we skip — the next read's `_ensure_index` extends\n481\t                    # the active over the gap bytes AND this one. `_index_epoch` is intentionally left stale\n482\t                    # so that next read refreshes it (its extend then finds nothing to do — O(1)).\n483\t                    with self._index_lock:\n484\t                        active_str = str(active)\n485\t                        if self._seg_scanned.get(active_str) == offset:\n486\t                            self._offsets[entry.seq] = (active_str, offset)\n487\t                            if entry.seq > self._max_seq:\n488\t                                self._max_seq = entry.seq\n489\t                            self._seg_scanned[active_str] = offset + len(line.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n490\t                self._last = entry\n491\t        return entry.seq\n492\t\n493\t    # --- read ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n494\t    def _open_segment(self, p: Path):\n495\t        \"\"\"Open a segment for a forward byte read. Plaintext in Slices 1-3; gzip-transparent in Slice 4.\"\"\"\n496\t        return p.open(\"rb\")\n497\t\n498\t    def iter_records(self, *, since_seq: int = -1) -> Iterator[SpineRecord]:\n499\t        \"\"\"Records with seq > `since_seq`, in seq order, ACROSS ALL SEGMENTS (sealed segments then the\n500\t        active) as ONE contiguous chain, so every full-scanning consumer keeps seeing the whole log. For\n501\t        since_seq >= 0 the index seeks straight to the first wanted line in its owning segment\n502\t        (O(records-returned), not O(spine)); a full read (since_seq < 0) streams every segment from the\n503\t        first. A line that fails to parse or lacks required keys is SKIPPED (a torn active tail can't crash\n504\t        the read; a torn MIDDLE line becomes a seq gap that verify() fails on). RAISES SpineError if the\n505\t        manifest references a segment that cannot be read — a read must NEVER silently yield a short chain\n506\t        (invariant 15: no state-scanner fail-open).\"\"\"\n507\t        segs = self._segments_in_order()                 # RAISES on a missing referenced segment\n508\t        if not segs:\n509\t            return\n510\t        start_i, start_off = self._start_locus(since_seq, segs)\n511\t        for i in range(start_i, len(segs)):\n512\t            p = segs[i]\n513\t            try:\n514\t                f = self._open_segment(p)\n515\t            except FileNotFoundError as e:               # referenced but vanished mid-read\n516\t                raise SpineError(f\"spine segment vanished during read: {p}\") from e\n517\t            with f:\n518\t                if i == start_i and start_off:\n519\t                    f.seek(start_off)\n520\t                for raw in f:\n521\t                    if not raw.strip():\n522\t                        continue\n523\t                    try:\n524\t                        rec = SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(raw))\n525\t                    except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n526\t                        _log.warning(\"spine: skipping malformed line during iter_records (%s)\", p)\n527\t                        continue\n528\t                    if rec.seq > since_seq:\n529\t                        yield rec\n530\t\n531\t    def get(self, seq: int) -> SpineRecord | None:\n532\t        \"\"\"A single record by seq — O(1) via the segment-aware index (resolves the OWNING segment + offset),\n533\t        byte-identical to a full scan. Falls back to a bounded forward scan (which spans segments) when the\n534\t        seq is not indexed (beyond the tip, or a corrupt line was skipped).\"\"\"\n535\t        if seq < 0:\n536\t            return None\n537\t        self._ensure_index()\n538\t        with self._index_lock:\n539\t            loc = self._offsets.get(seq)\n540\t        if loc is not None:\n541\t            path_str, off = loc\n542\t            try:\n543\t                with self._open_segment(Path(path_str)) as f:\n544\t                    f.seek(off)\n545\t                    raw = f.readline()\n546\t                rec = SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(raw))\n547\t                if rec.seq == seq:\n548\t                    return rec\n549\t            except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n550\t                pass                                     # stale/moved offset — fall through to the scan\n551\t        for r in self.iter_records(since_seq=seq - 1):\n552\t            if r.seq == seq:\n553\t                return r\n554\t            if r.seq > seq:\n555\t                break\n556\t        return None\n557\t\n558\t    def tail(self, n: int) -> list[SpineRecord]:\n559\t        \"\"\"The last `n` records, walking segments backward from the ACTIVE across the seam (O(n) bytes,\n560\t        NOT O(spine)) — a bounded RECENT-window read on a large spine. Fewer than `n` if the spine is\n561\t        shorter. FIXES B3: the pre-Slice-1 version read only the active file, so right after a rotation it\n562\t        returned < n. NOTE (unchanged): a bounded window collapses a rapid recent replay flood but does NOT\n563\t        bound AGGREGATE replay bloat — pair `tail()`-based dedup with a record-time freshness gate.\"\"\"\n564\t        if n <= 0:\n565\t            return []\n566\t        recs: list[SpineRecord] = []\n567\t        for p in reversed(self._segments_in_order()):\n568\t            recs = self._read_segment_tail(p, n - len(recs)) + recs\n569\t            if len(recs) >= n:\n570\t                break\n571\t        return recs[-n:]\n572\t\n573\t    def _read_segment_tail(self, p: Path, k: int) -> list[SpineRecord]:\n574\t        \"\"\"The last `k` records of a single segment (seek-from-end, O(k) bytes). Plaintext in Slices 1-3.\"\"\"\n575\t        if k <= 0 or not p.exists():\n576\t            return []\n577\t        with p.open(\"rb\") as f:\n578\t            f.seek(0, os.SEEK_END)\n579\t            pos = f.tell()\n580\t            buf = b\"\"\n581\t            while pos > 0 and buf.count(b\"\\n\") <= k:\n582\t                step = min(65536, pos)\n583\t                pos -= step\n584\t                f.seek(pos)\n585\t                buf = f.read(step) + buf\n586\t        out: list[SpineRecord] = []\n587\t        for ln in [x for x in buf.split(b\"\\n\") if x.strip()][-k:]:\n588\t            try:\n589\t                out.append(SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(ln)))\n590\t            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n591\t                continue\n592\t        return out\n593\t\n594\t    @property\n595\t    def next_seq(self) -> int:\n596\t        return (self._last.seq + 1) if self._last else 0\n597\t\n598\t    def count(self) -> int:\n599\t        return sum(1 for _ in self.iter_records())\n600\t\n601\t    # --- integrity ----------------------------------------------------------------\n602\t    def entries(self) -> list[ChainEntry]:\n603\t        return [\n604\t            ChainEntry(seq=r.seq, prev_hash=r.prev_hash, cert_digest=r.cert_digest, entry_hash=r.entry_hash)\n605\t            for r in self.iter_records()\n606\t        ]\n607\t\n608\t    def verify(self) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n609\t        \"\"\"Two-layer UNKEYED integrity: (1) BINDING — each record's payload still hashes to its\n610\t        stored cert_digest (catches silent payload edits); (2) CHAIN — the entries link cleanly\n611\t        (catches delete/reorder/entry tamper). This proves internal CONSISTENCY, not authenticity:\n612\t        a naive payload edit fails (1), and a mid-chain digest edit cascades an entry_hash/prev_hash\n613\t        break caught by (2) — BUT a writer who recomputes cert_digest+entry_hash for the tip (no\n614\t        successor to cascade into) or forward-cascades a fork produces a self-consistent chain that\n615\t        passes here. Resistance to a recompute-capable writer is the owner-SIGNED head's job\n616\t        (`checkpoint.verify_checkpoint`, Ed25519 + monotonic last_seq). Use this for corruption/\n617\t        naive-tamper detection; use the signed head for tamper-EVIDENCE.\"\"\"\n618\t        entries: list[ChainEntry] = []\n619\t        for r in self.iter_records():\n620\t            content = {\n621\t                \"scope\": r.scope, \"kind\": r.kind, \"source\": r.source, \"actor\": r.actor,\n622\t                \"payload\": r.payload, \"parent_id\": r.parent_id, \"supersedes_id\": r.supersedes_id,\n623\t            }\n624\t            if digest_payload(content) != r.cert_digest:\n625\t                return False, f\"binding break at seq {r.seq}: payload does not match cert_digest (record tampered)\"\n626\t            entries.append(ChainEntry(seq=r.seq, prev_hash=r.prev_hash, cert_digest=r.cert_digest, entry_hash=r.entry_hash))\n627\t        return verify_chain(entries)\n628\t\n629\t    @staticmethod\n630\t    def _entry_from_line(line: str | None) -> \"ChainEntry | None\":\n631\t        if not line:\n632\t            return None\n633\t        d = json.loads(line)\n634\t        return ChainEntry(seq=d[\"seq\"], prev_hash=d[\"prev_hash\"], cert_digest=d[\"cert_digest\"],\n635\t                          entry_hash=d[\"entry_hash\"])\n636\t\n637\t    def _read_last_entry(self) -> ChainEntry | None:\n638\t        \"\"\"The chain tip = the last valid record of the ACTIVE segment. If the active is EMPTY (a freshly\n639\t        rotated segment before its first append), the tip is the last SEALED segment's real last record\n640\t        (the seam boundary), so next_seq never regresses / reuses a seq across a rotation (invariant 13).\n641\t        `_last_nonempty_line` skips a torn/garbage tail, so a crash mid-write can't block a restart.\"\"\"\n642\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n643\t        if m is None:\n644\t            t = self._read_target()\n645\t            return self._entry_from_line(_last_nonempty_line(t) if t.exists() else None)\n646\t        act = m.active()\n647\t        if act is not None:\n648\t            ap = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n649\t            line = _last_nonempty_line(ap) if ap.exists() else None\n650\t            if line:\n651\t                return self._entry_from_line(line)\n652\t        for seg in reversed(m.sealed_in_order()):        # active empty/absent -> last sealed segment's tail\n653\t            sp = self._layout.seg_path(seg)\n654\t            line = _last_nonempty_line(sp) if sp.exists() else None\n655\t            if line:\n656\t                return self._entry_from_line(line)\n657\t        return None\n658\t\n659\t    # --- segment-aware seq -> (segment, byte-offset) index ------------------------\n660\t    def _start_locus(self, since_seq: int, segs: list[Path]) -> tuple[int, int]:\n661\t        \"\"\"(starting segment index in `segs`, byte offset within it) so a forward read yields exactly\n662\t        seq > since_seq. A full read (since_seq < 0) → (0, 0). Otherwise the index seeks to seq\n663\t        (since_seq + 1) in its owning segment; if that seq is beyond the tip, return a past-the-end index\n664\t        so nothing is yielded; if not resolvable (below the min, or a skipped/corrupt gap), fall back to a\n665\t        safe full span from segment 0.\"\"\"\n666\t        if since_seq < 0:\n667\t            return (0, 0)\n668\t        self._ensure_index()\n669\t        want = since_seq + 1\n670\t        with self._index_lock:\n671\t            loc = self._offsets.get(want)\n672\t            max_seq = self._max_seq\n673\t        if loc is not None:\n674\t            path_str, off = loc\n675\t            for i, p in enumerate(segs):\n676\t                if str(p) == path_str:\n677\t                    return (i, off)\n678\t            return (0, 0)                           # segment set changed under us → safe full span\n679\t        if want > max_seq:\n680\t            return (len(segs), 0)                   # nothing beyond the tip → yield nothing\n681\t        return (0, 0)                               # below min / gap → safe full span\n682\t\n683\t    def _ensure_index(self) -> None:\n684\t        \"\"\"Build/reconcile the segment-aware index lazily, keyed on the epoch (`change_token`). On an epoch\n685\t        change (an append grew the active, or a rotation/migration changed the segment set) reconcile each\n686\t        segment: the ACTIVE extends over the new bytes (or re-scans if it shrank in place — a truncation);\n687\t        a SEALED segment is immutable and indexed exactly once. Thread-safe under `_index_lock`.\"\"\"\n688\t        tok = self.change_token()\n689\t        try:\n690\t            segs = self._segments_in_order()\n691\t        except SpineError:\n692\t            return                                  # a missing segment → reads raise; don't poison the index\n693\t        active_str = str(segs[-1]) if segs else None\n694\t        with self._index_lock:\n695\t            if tok == self._index_epoch:\n696\t                return\n697\t            for p in segs:\n698\t                ps = str(p)\n699\t                try:\n700\t                    size = p.stat().st_size\n701\t                except OSError:\n702\t                    continue\n703\t                scanned = self._seg_scanned.get(ps, 0)\n704\t                if ps == active_str:\n705\t                    if size > scanned:\n706\t                        self._scan_segment(ps, p, scanned, size)     # extend the active over new bytes\n707\t                    elif size < scanned:                             # active shrank in place (truncation)\n708\t                        self._purge_segment(ps)\n709\t                        self._scan_segment(ps, p, 0, size)\n710\t                elif scanned < size:                                 # sealed: immutable → index once\n711\t                    self._scan_segment(ps, p, scanned, size)\n712\t            self._index_epoch = tok\n713\t\n714\t    def _purge_segment(self, ps: str) -> None:\n715\t        \"\"\"Drop a segment's offsets (the active shrank in place — a truncation). MUST hold `_index_lock`.\"\"\"\n716\t        self._offsets = {s: loc for s, loc in self._offsets.items() if loc[0] != ps}\n717\t        self._seg_scanned.pop(ps, None)\n718\t        self._max_seq = max(self._offsets, default=-1)\n719\t\n720\t    def _scan_segment(self, ps: str, p: Path, start: int, size: int) -> None:\n721\t        \"\"\"Index every COMPLETE (newline-terminated) record line in bytes [start, size) of segment `p` as\n722\t        `_offsets[seq] = (ps, line_offset)`. MUST hold `_index_lock`. A malformed complete line is left OUT\n723\t        (so a mid-file gap still surfaces via verify()); a trailing partial line is left for the next\n724\t        extend.\"\"\"\n725\t        if size <= start:\n726\t            return\n727\t        with p.open(\"rb\") as f:\n728\t            f.seek(start)\n729\t            chunk = f.read(size - start)\n730\t        consumed = 0\n731\t        while True:\n732\t            nl = chunk.find(b\"\\n\", consumed)\n733\t            if nl == -1:\n734\t                break                                       # trailing partial line — not complete yet\n735\t            raw = chunk[consumed:nl]\n736\t            line_off = start + consumed\n737\t            consumed = nl + 1\n738\t            if not raw.strip():\n739\t                continue\n740\t            try:\n741\t                d = json.loads(raw)\n742\t                if not all(k in d for k in _REQUIRED_KEYS):\n743\t                    raise KeyError\n744\t                seq = d[\"seq\"]\n745\t            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n746\t                continue                                    # corrupt complete line — do not index it\n747\t            self._offsets[seq] = (ps, line_off)\n748\t            if seq > self._max_seq:\n749\t                self._max_seq = seq\n750\t        self._seg_scanned[ps] = start + consumed\n751\t"}]},"uuid":"80a414a3-c07b-4d34-af50-fd2bac729aea","timestamp":"2026-07-19T17:44:05.741Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"77a41459-82f9-4e5b-9539-ad35de25f609","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"1ab376cf-798d-414b-b6c3-91cb18763b7e","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a477a9a919faf465c","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdBqAs75VMmCwsGmQtGZn","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01UwwEuZ3DpVmqiQgUAfEB4B","name":"Read","input":{"file_path":"/home/kali/sigil/sigil/spine/manifest.py"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":16081,"cache_read_input_tokens":51534,"output_tokens":436,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":16081},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":436,"cache_read_input_tokens":51534,"cache_creation_input_tokens":16081,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":16081,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdBqApz5Xok32g8Mi61DX","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"525dbdd5-8b06-4b33-b71e-37e34f661f0d","timestamp":"2026-07-19T17:44:14.663Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"525dbdd5-8b06-4b33-b71e-37e34f661f0d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a1bd755-fe55-49e4-aa04-1c9cb10eb027","agentId":"a477a9a919faf465c","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01UwwEuZ3DpVmqiQgUAfEB4B","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"The spine's segment MANIFEST — the sole linearization point for the retain-all segment set.\n2\t\n3\tThe spine is stored as a sequence of size-bounded, immutable **sealed** segments plus exactly one\n4\t**active** (append) segment; the manifest names them in seq order. It is deliberately UNSIGNED and\n5\tNON-LOAD-BEARING: record order, count, and every tamper decision are re-derived from the segment BYTES\n6\t(via `verify_chain`/`classify_head`), never from the manifest's convenience fields. A doctored manifest\n7\ttherefore fails CLOSED — dropping/reordering an interior segment breaks `prev_hash`/seq contiguity, a\n8\tdropped tail shortens `entries()` (the signed head's `n < entry_count` gate fires), a dropped front fails\n9\tthe genesis check. (A future signed-manifest tier would set `manifest_sig` — reserved `null` — because it\n10\twould then attest pruned ranges; retain-all needs no such trust.)\n11\t\n12\t`SpineLayout` derives the whole on-disk layout from the spine data-file path a `SpineStore` is constructed\n13\twith, so a store on a temp path is fully isolated. This module does no locking — the caller (`SpineStore`)\n14\tholds the cross-process flock while reading/writing/migrating.\n15\t\"\"\"\n16\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n17\t\n18\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n19\tfrom pathlib import PurePosixPath, Path\n20\t\n21\tfrom pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field, field_validator\n22\t\n23\tfrom ..reuse.chain import _GENESIS_PREV\n24\tfrom .atomicio import atomic_write_text\n25\t\n26\tSEGMENT_STEM = \"seg-\"\n27\t_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1\n28\t\n29\t\n30\tdef segment_filename(seg_id: int, codec: str = \"none\") -> str:\n31\t    \"\"\"Zero-padded segment file name; `.gz` suffix when gzip-sealed (Slice 4).\"\"\"\n32\t    base = f\"{SEGMENT_STEM}{seg_id:08d}.jsonl\"\n33\t    return base + \".gz\" if codec == \"gzip\" else base\n34\t\n35\t\n36\tclass Segment(BaseModel):\n37\t    \"\"\"One segment in the manifest. `first_prev_hash`/`boundary_hash` are the chain-linkage the reader\n38\t    CROSS-CHECKS against the segment's real first/last records — they are convenience, not authority.\"\"\"\n39\t    model_config = ConfigDict(extra=\"ignore\")   # forward-compatible: tolerate fields a newer writer added\n40\t    id: int = Field(ge=0)\n41\t    file: str                                    # path RELATIVE to the spine dir, e.g. \"segments/seg-00000000.jsonl\"\n42\t    codec: str = \"none\"                          # \"none\" | \"gzip\"\n43\t    sealed: bool = False\n44\t    first_seq: int = Field(ge=0)\n45\t    last_seq: int | None = None                  # null while active\n46\t    count: int | None = None                     # null while active; CONVENIENCE ONLY — never a tamper input\n47\t    first_prev_hash: str = _GENESIS_PREV         # == the prior segment's boundary_hash (seg 0 == genesis)\n48\t    boundary_hash: str | None = None             # == entry_hash(last_seq); null while active\n49\t    bytes: int = 0\n50\t    sha256: str | None = None                    # at-rest file integrity; NOT a chain digest\n51\t\n52\t    @field_validator(\"file\")\n53\t    @classmethod\n54\t    def _file_is_contained_relative(cls, v: str) -> str:\n55\t        \"\"\"A segment `file` must be a plain relative path under the spine dir — never absolute and never\n56\t        `..`-escaping. `get()`/`iter_records()`/`tail()` open segment files WITHOUT running verify(), so a\n57\t        doctored manifest pointing `file` at `/etc/passwd` or `../../secret` would otherwise surface that\n58\t        file's bytes AS spine records. Reject it at the model boundary so the read path stays fail-closed\n59\t        even though the manifest itself is unsigned.\"\"\"\n60\t        pp = PurePosixPath(v)\n61\t        if not v or pp.is_absolute() or PurePosixPath(v.replace(\"\\\\\", \"/\")).is_absolute() or \"..\" in pp.parts:\n62\t            raise ValueError(f\"segment file must be a contained relative path, got {v!r}\")\n63\t        return v\n64\t\n65\t\n66\tclass Manifest(BaseModel):\n67\t    model_config = ConfigDict(extra=\"ignore\")\n68\t    schema_version: int = _SCHEMA_VERSION\n69\t    generation: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)     # monotonic epoch; ++ on EVERY swap — the unified change token\n70\t    scope: str = \"\"\n71\t    segments: list[Segment] = Field(default_factory=list)\n72\t    manifest_sig: str | None = None              # reserved for the deferred signed-manifest tier\n73\t\n74\t    def active(self) -> Segment | None:\n75\t        \"\"\"The single unsealed (append) segment, if any. There is at most one.\"\"\"\n76\t        act = [s for s in self.segments if not s.sealed]\n77\t        return act[-1] if act else None\n78\t\n79\t    def sealed_in_order(self) -> list[Segment]:\n80\t        return sorted((s for s in self.segments if s.sealed), key=lambda s: s.first_seq)\n81\t\n82\t    def ordered(self) -> list[Segment]:\n83\t        \"\"\"All segments in seq order (sealed by first_seq, then the active tail).\"\"\"\n84\t        out = self.sealed_in_order()\n85\t        act = self.active()\n86\t        if act is not None:\n87\t            out.append(act)\n88\t        return out\n89\t\n90\t\n91\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n92\tclass SpineLayout:\n93\t    \"\"\"The on-disk layout derived from a spine data-file path (…/spine.jsonl). Every artifact is\n94\t    NAMESPACED BY THE DATA FILE'S STEM (`spine.manifest.json`, `spine.lock`, `spine.segments/`) so two\n95\t    stores whose data files merely SHARE a directory — e.g. the `tempfile.mktemp()` idiom that drops files\n96\t    straight into /tmp — never collide on a manifest/lock/segments set. Without this, one migrated store's\n97\t    `manifest.json` would be read by every other store in that directory and silently steer it onto the\n98\t    wrong segment (a real cross-store contamination the review caught).\"\"\"\n99\t    data_path: Path            # the legacy/identity path (…/spine.jsonl) — the stable lock/identity token\n100\t    spine_dir: Path\n101\t    segments_dir: Path\n102\t    manifest_path: Path\n103\t    lockfile_path: Path\n104\t    trash_dir: Path\n105\t\n106\t    @classmethod\n107\t    def for_path(cls, data_path: Path | str) -> \"SpineLayout\":\n108\t        p = Path(data_path)\n109\t        d = p.parent\n110\t        ns = p.stem or p.name       # \"spine.jsonl\" -> \"spine\"; namespaces every derived artifact\n111\t        return cls(\n112\t            data_path=p, spine_dir=d, segments_dir=d / f\"{ns}.segments\",\n113\t            manifest_path=d / f\"{ns}.manifest.json\", lockfile_path=d / f\"{ns}.lock\", trash_dir=d / f\"{ns}.trash\",\n114\t        )\n115\t\n116\t    def seg_path(self, seg: Segment) -> Path:\n117\t        \"\"\"Absolute path of a segment's file (its `file` is stored relative to the spine dir). The\n118\t        `Segment.file` validator guarantees it is a plain relative path that cannot escape spine_dir.\"\"\"\n119\t        return self.spine_dir / seg.file\n120\t\n121\t    def segment_rel(self, seg_id: int, codec: str = \"none\") -> str:\n122\t        \"\"\"The manifest `file` value (relative to spine_dir) for a segment id — under the namespaced\n123\t        segments dir, e.g. 'spine.segments/seg-00000000.jsonl'.\"\"\"\n124\t        return f\"{self.segments_dir.name}/{segment_filename(seg_id, codec)}\"\n125\t\n126\t\n127\tdef read_manifest(layout: SpineLayout) -> Manifest | None:\n128\t    \"\"\"Load the manifest, or None if absent. A present-but-unparseable manifest RAISES (fail closed) —\n129\t    the atomic write path guarantees it is never torn, so a parse failure means genuine corruption, not a\n130\t    partial write, and must never be silently treated as 'no manifest' (which would strand the segments).\"\"\"\n131\t    mp = layout.manifest_path\n132\t    if not mp.exists():\n133\t        return None\n134\t    return Manifest.model_validate_json(mp.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n135\t\n136\t\n137\tdef write_manifest(layout: SpineLayout, manifest: Manifest) -> None:\n138\t    \"\"\"Atomically publish the manifest (temp→fsync→os.replace→dir-fsync). THE cutover commit instant.\"\"\"\n139\t    layout.manifest_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n140\t    atomic_write_text(layout.manifest_path, manifest.model_dump_json(), prefix=\".manifest-\")\n141\t\n142\t\n143\tdef initial_manifest(scope: str, *, active_file: str, first_seq: int = 0,\n144\t                     first_prev_hash: str = _GENESIS_PREV) -> Manifest:\n145\t    \"\"\"A single-active-segment manifest (generation 0) — the shape produced by a fresh store or a\n146\t    migration of an existing single-file spine.\"\"\"\n147\t    return Manifest(\n148\t        generation=0, scope=scope,\n149\t        segments=[Segment(id=0, file=active_file, codec=\"none\", sealed=False,\n150\t                          first_seq=first_seq, first_prev_hash=first_prev_hash)],\n151\t    )\n152\t"}]},"uuid":"d77eb4e3-2eb7-485d-b92e-3a6ec5125c65","timestamp":"2026-07-19T17:44:14.684Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"525dbdd5-8b06-4b33-b71e-37e34f661f0d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"0d2c6873-b400-4e28-b744-94b6c7a794b7","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a1bd755-fe55-49e4-aa04-1c9cb10eb027","agentId":"a477a9a919faf465c","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018D1hEzyPdWL1DVogGh6Tw2","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"SpineTailer (Phase 7, P0.2) — a live tail over the append-only, poll-only spine with an HONEST,\n2\ttwo-layer integrity model (red-pen-hardened: unkeyed hashes alone cannot resist a writer who can\n3\trecompute them):\n4\t\n5\t  • `integrity_ok` (CHEAP, per-poll, unkeyed) — per-record binding+derivation (`verify_record`) plus\n6\t    cross-record LINKAGE (this record's `prev_hash` == the last emitted `entry_hash`). This catches\n7\t    ACCIDENTAL corruption, a naive stale-field tamper, a deletion/reorder/gap. It does NOT resist a\n8\t    file-writer who recomputes cert_digest+entry_hash (a tip tamper or a forward-cascaded fork stays\n9\t    internally consistent) — so it is necessary but not sufficient, and is never presented as proof\n10\t    of authenticity on its own.\n11\t\n12\t  • `anchored` (KEYED, via `check_anchor`) — the owner-SIGNED head (`checkpoint.classify_head` /\n13\t    `verify_head`, Ed25519 1-of-1 trust root, monotonic `last_seq`) notarizes the prefix\n14\t    [0 .. signed_last_seq]. A recompute-fork or truncation AT OR BELOW the signed head is\n15\t    unforgeable without the owner key and is caught here; a monotonic high-water catches a rollback\n16\t    of even the un-notarized tail. Records with seq > signed_last_seq are the FRESH, not-yet-keyed\n17\t    tail — emitted `anchored=False` (well-formed but not tamper-proof), keyed at the next `sigil sign`.\n18\t\n19\tA consumer (UI/mobile) shows `integrity_ok=False` as \"integrity broken\", and treats only\n20\t`anchored=True` records as owner-notarized truth; the un-notarized tail is labelled as such, never\n21\tdressed up as proven. One primitive, two consumers — the UI SSE feed and the mobile push notifier —\n22\tboth drive `poll()` on a tick and fan events out via `Broadcaster`.\"\"\"\n23\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n24\t\n25\tfrom collections import deque\n26\tfrom typing import Optional\n27\t\n28\tfrom ..config import HEAD_PATH\n29\tfrom ..reuse import SignedChainHead\n30\tfrom ..reuse.chain import _GENESIS_PREV\n31\tfrom .checkpoint import classify_head, trust_root\n32\tfrom .store import SpineStore\n33\tfrom .verify import verify_record\n34\t\n35\t\n36\tdef _shape(record, *, integrity_ok: bool, integrity_reason: str, anchored: bool) -> dict:\n37\t    \"\"\"The cited atom shape emitted to consumers. `integrity_ok` = unkeyed well-formedness;\n38\t    `anchored` = covered by the owner-signed head (keyed). `text` is best-effort, never fabricated.\"\"\"\n39\t    return {\n40\t        \"seq\": record.seq, \"kind\": record.kind, \"source\": record.source, \"actor\": record.actor,\n41\t        \"ts\": record.ts, \"entry_hash\": record.entry_hash, \"text\": record.text(),\n42\t        \"integrity_ok\": integrity_ok, \"integrity_reason\": integrity_reason, \"anchored\": anchored,\n43\t    }\n44\t\n45\t\n46\tclass SpineTailer:\n47\t    def __init__(self, store: Optional[SpineStore] = None, *, since_seq: int = -1,\n48\t                 signed_head: Optional[SignedChainHead] = None, tr=None):\n49\t        \"\"\"Emit records with seq > `since_seq`. `signed_head`/`tr` may be injected (tests); otherwise\n50\t        the owner-signed head is loaded from `HEAD_PATH` + the persisted trust root.\"\"\"\n51\t        self.store = store or SpineStore()\n52\t        self.cursor = since_seq\n53\t        if since_seq < 0:\n54\t            self._last_hash = _GENESIS_PREV\n55\t        else:\n56\t            anchor = self.store.get(since_seq)\n57\t            self._last_hash = anchor.entry_hash if anchor else _GENESIS_PREV\n58\t        self._injected_head = signed_head\n59\t        self._injected_tr = tr\n60\t        self._high_water = -1                           # monotonic anti-rollback water mark\n61\t        self._signed_last_seq = -1\n62\t        self.check_anchor()                             # establish the initial keyed anchor (reads disk)\n63\t\n64\t    def poll(self) -> list[dict]:\n65\t        \"\"\"Newly-appended records (seq > cursor), each with the cheap `integrity_ok` verdict and the\n66\t        `anchored` (keyed) flag from the last `check_anchor`. On an integrity failure the event is\n67\t        emitted flagged (not dropped); the cursor still advances so the feed never re-emits or stalls.\"\"\"\n68\t        out: list[dict] = []\n69\t        for r in self.store.iter_records(since_seq=self.cursor):\n70\t            atom_ok, reason = verify_record(r)\n71\t            if r.prev_hash != self._last_hash:\n72\t                ok = False\n73\t                reason = f\"linkage break at seq {r.seq}: prev_hash != last emitted entry_hash (rewind/fork/gap)\"\n74\t            else:\n75\t                ok = atom_ok\n76\t            out.append(_shape(r, integrity_ok=ok, integrity_reason=reason,\n77\t                              anchored=(r.seq <= self._signed_last_seq)))\n78\t            self.cursor = r.seq\n79\t            self._last_hash = r.entry_hash\n80\t        return out\n81\t\n82\t    def check_anchor(self) -> dict:\n83\t        \"\"\"The KEYED verdict. Verify the owner-signed head over the current chain (unforgeable without\n84\t        the owner key): a recompute-fork or truncation at/below the signed head → `anchor_ok=False`;\n85\t        a monotonic high-water rollback of even the un-notarized tail → `rollback` set. Updates\n86\t        `signed_last_seq` so `poll()` can flag records beyond it as the un-notarized tail.\"\"\"\n87\t        entries = self.store.entries()                  # FRESH read from disk (not the cached head)\n88\t        head_seq = entries[-1].seq if entries else -1\n89\t        rollback = None\n90\t        if head_seq < self._high_water:\n91\t            rollback = f\"ROLLBACK: on-disk head seq {head_seq} < monotonic high-water {self._high_water}\"\n92\t        else:\n93\t            self._high_water = head_seq\n94\t        head, tr = self._resolve_head()\n95\t        if head is None:\n96\t            self._signed_last_seq = -1\n97\t            return {\"anchor_ok\": False, \"signed_last_seq\": -1, \"rollback\": rollback,\n98\t                    \"reason\": \"no signed checkpoint — the live tail is well-formed but UN-NOTARIZED \"\n99\t                              \"(run `sigil sign` to anchor)\"}\n100\t        try:\n101\t            ok, reason = classify_head(head, entries, tr)\n102\t        except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 — a malformed head must FAIL closed, never crash the feed\n103\t            self._signed_last_seq = -1\n104\t            return {\"anchor_ok\": False, \"signed_last_seq\": -1, \"rollback\": rollback,\n105\t                    \"reason\": f\"anchor unverifiable ({e})\"}\n106\t        self._signed_last_seq = head.last_seq if ok else -1\n107\t        return {\"anchor_ok\": ok and rollback is None, \"signed_last_seq\": self._signed_last_seq,\n108\t                \"rollback\": rollback, \"reason\": reason}\n109\t\n110\t    def _resolve_head(self):\n111\t        if self._injected_head is not None:\n112\t            return self._injected_head, self._injected_tr\n113\t        try:\n114\t            if not HEAD_PATH.exists():\n115\t                return None, None\n116\t            head = SignedChainHead.model_validate_json(HEAD_PATH.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n117\t            return head, trust_root()\n118\t        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — no/broken head → unkeyed live tail (honest, fail-closed)\n119\t            return None, None\n120\t\n121\t\n122\tclass Broadcaster:\n123\t    \"\"\"Fan-out of tail events to N subscribers with bounded per-subscriber queues. On overflow the\n124\t    oldest events are dropped and the subscriber is marked `lagged` so it resyncs from the cursor\n125\t    (never a fabricated 'you saw everything').\"\"\"\n126\t    def __init__(self, maxlen: int = 512):\n127\t        self.maxlen = maxlen\n128\t        self._subs: dict[int, deque] = {}\n129\t        self._lagged: dict[int, bool] = {}\n130\t        self._next_id = 0\n131\t\n132\t    def subscribe(self) -> int:\n133\t        sid = self._next_id\n134\t        self._next_id += 1\n135\t        self._subs[sid] = deque(maxlen=self.maxlen)\n136\t        self._lagged[sid] = False\n137\t        return sid\n138\t\n139\t    def unsubscribe(self, sid: int) -> None:\n140\t        self._subs.pop(sid, None)\n141\t        self._lagged.pop(sid, None)\n142\t\n143\t    def publish(self, events: list[dict]) -> None:\n144\t        for sid, q in self._subs.items():\n145\t            for ev in events:\n146\t                if len(q) == self.maxlen:        # would drop the oldest → the subscriber lagged\n147\t                    self._lagged[sid] = True\n148\t                q.append(ev)\n149\t\n150\t    def drain(self, sid: int) -> tuple[list[dict], bool]:\n151\t        \"\"\"Return (events, lagged) and clear both. `lagged=True` tells the consumer to resync.\"\"\"\n152\t        q = self._subs.get(sid)\n153\t        if q is None:\n154\t            return [], False\n155\t        events = list(q)\n156\t        q.clear()\n157\t        lagged = self._lagged.get(sid, False)\n158\t        self._lagged[sid] = False\n159\t        return events, lagged\n160\t"}]},"uuid":"b16b3ddd-34a3-4897-ad63-f7f16d4702f2","timestamp":"2026-07-19T17:45:41.534Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0d2c6873-b400-4e28-b744-94b6c7a794b7","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"bcc6eaf0-eac7-4d9d-9d71-628fa59217dd","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a1bd755-fe55-49e4-aa04-1c9cb10eb027","agentId":"a477a9a919faf465c","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01P7JNswudietNTSB9STwCUb","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"The kill switch (SIGIL §5) — a single latch that halts the agent mesh while leaving perception and\n2\tmemory-read (A0 observe) alive. State is append-only on the spine.\n3\t\n4\tASYMMETRIC AUTHENTICATION (Phase 6 red-pen RP-2): halting is always the safe direction, so ANY\n5\tengage event halts (a nuisance forged-engage is at worst a fail-safe DoS). UN-halting is the\n6\tdangerous direction, so a RELEASE is honored ONLY if it is signed by the owner key and verifies\n7\tagainst the trusted pubkey — a forged release can never revive a halted mesh. Read fresh per decision\n8\tso a release (or engage) takes effect immediately.\"\"\"\n9\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n10\t\n11\timport threading\n12\tfrom pathlib import Path\n13\tfrom typing import Optional\n14\t\n15\tfrom .authn import signed_payload, verify_signed\n16\tfrom .identity import owner_keypair, owner_pubkey\n17\t\n18\tSIGNAL = \"governor.killswitch\"\n19\t_CORE = (\"signal\", \"state\")\n20\t\n21\t# FIX 4 (audit CRITICAL): `is_engaged()` full-scans the spine on EVERY governor decision, so a batch of\n22\t# proposals is O(proposals × spine). Cache the authoritative verdict keyed by (resolved spine path,\n23\t# trusted pubkey) with the store's ROTATION-AWARE CHANGE TOKEN it was computed at. Every engage/release\n24\t# APPENDS a record → the token changes → the cache invalidates and we re-run the real, owner-signed-\n25\t# release-verifying scan. A matching token ⇒ no new records ⇒ the cached verdict is exact. The token\n26\t# (invariant 9 / A4) keys on the manifest generation + the resolved ACTIVE segment (size, inode), NOT a\n27\t# bare `store.path.stat()` — which would raise/freeze once a migration renames spine.jsonl away and then\n28\t# serve a STALE (un-halting) verdict indefinitely. Keyed on the pubkey too, so instances with different\n29\t# trust roots never share an entry. Shared across ALL callers on a path.\n30\t_STATE_CACHE: dict[tuple[str, Optional[str]], tuple[tuple, bool]] = {}\n31\t_CACHE_GUARD = threading.Lock()\n32\t\n33\t\n34\tclass KillSwitch:\n35\t    def __init__(self, store, *, owner_key=None, trusted_pubkey: Optional[str] = None):\n36\t        self.store = store\n37\t        self.owner_key = owner_key if owner_key is not None else owner_keypair()\n38\t        self.trusted_pubkey = trusted_pubkey if trusted_pubkey is not None else owner_pubkey()\n39\t\n40\t    def engage(self, *, by: str = \"owner\", reason: str = \"\") -> int:\n41\t        core = {\"signal\": SIGNAL, \"state\": \"engaged\"}\n42\t        payload = {**signed_payload(core, self.owner_key), \"by\": by, \"reason\": reason,\n43\t                   \"tier\": \"A0\", \"decision\": \"auto\"}\n44\t        return self.store.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"governor\", actor=\"WARDEN\", payload=payload)\n45\t\n46\t    def release(self, *, by: str = \"owner\", reason: str = \"\") -> int:\n47\t        core = {\"signal\": SIGNAL, \"state\": \"released\"}\n48\t        payload = {**signed_payload(core, self.owner_key), \"by\": by, \"reason\": reason,\n49\t                   \"tier\": \"A0\", \"decision\": \"auto\"}\n50\t        return self.store.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"governor\", actor=\"WARDEN\", payload=payload)\n51\t\n52\t    def is_engaged(self) -> bool:\n53\t        \"\"\"Cheap, correct kill-switch verdict (FIX 4). Cheap when the spine is unchanged since the last\n54\t        authoritative scan (a matching rotation-aware change token); a changed token — a new engage/release\n55\t        record, or a migration/rotation that moved the active segment — re-runs the real scan below and\n56\t        refreshes the shared cache. Semantics are IDENTICAL to a fresh scan, and (unlike a bare file-size\n57\t        check) a migration that renames spine.jsonl away can never freeze the token and serve a stale\n58\t        un-halting verdict.\"\"\"\n59\t        key = (str(Path(self.store.path).resolve()), self.trusted_pubkey)\n60\t        token = self.store.change_token()\n61\t        with _CACHE_GUARD:\n62\t            cached = _STATE_CACHE.get(key)\n63\t            if cached is not None and cached[0] == token:\n64\t                return cached[1]\n65\t        engaged = self._scan_engaged()\n66\t        with _CACHE_GUARD:\n67\t            _STATE_CACHE[key] = (token, engaged)\n68\t        return engaged\n69\t\n70\t    def _scan_engaged(self) -> bool:\n71\t        \"\"\"The AUTHORITATIVE scan — unchanged semantics (not re-implemented): honor ANY engage (halting\n72\t        is fail-safe), and un-halt only on an OWNER-SIGNED release that verifies (fail-closed).\"\"\"\n73\t        engaged = False\n74\t        for r in self.store.iter_records():\n75\t            p = r.payload\n76\t            if p.get(\"signal\") != SIGNAL:\n77\t                continue\n78\t            state = p.get(\"state\")\n79\t            if state == \"engaged\":\n80\t                engaged = True                      # honor ANY engage — halting is fail-safe\n81\t            elif state == \"released\" and verify_signed(p, _CORE, self.trusted_pubkey):\n82\t                engaged = False                     # only an OWNER-SIGNED release un-halts (fail-closed)\n83\t        return engaged\n84\t"}]},"uuid":"ce43dcae-fc42-4db6-adfe-931336bf6a41","timestamp":"2026-07-19T17:45:41.709Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"bcc6eaf0-eac7-4d9d-9d71-628fa59217dd","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Use this to tell the owner how current/trustworthy\n111\t    their memory is before relying on it.\n112\t\n113\t    Args:\n114\t        verify: also run the (heavier) integrity + signed-head check (default true).\n115\t    \"\"\"\n116\t    store = SpineStore()\n117\t    vi = _idx()\n118\t    status: dict[str, Any] = {\n119\t        \"spine_records\": store.count(),\n120\t        \"next_seq\": store.next_seq,\n121\t        \"vectors_indexed\": vi.count(),\n122\t        \"last_indexed_seq\": vi.last_indexed_seq(),\n123\t        \"embedding_model\": EMBED_MODEL,\n124\t        \"qdrant_mode\": \"server\" if QDRANT_URL else \"local-embedded\",\n125\t    }\n126\t    if verify:\n127\t        ok, msg = store.verify()\n128\t        hok, hmsg = verify_checkpoint(store)\n129\t        status[\"chain\"] = {\"ok\": ok, \"detail\": msg}\n130\t        status[\"signed_head\"] = {\"ok\": hok, \"detail\": hmsg}\n131\t    return status\n132\t\n133\t\n134\t@mcp.tool()\n135\tdef graph_entity(name: str) -> dict[str, Any]:\n136\t    \"\"\"Look up a structural entity in the owner's memory graph and return it WITH its\n137\t    neighbours and spine anchors. Resolves `name` as a Project, then a Session id, a Commit\n138\t    hash prefix, or a Document path substring.
